## Document 011: 115年10月海關進出口貿易初步統計 (2026-11-09)

Extraction status: ok

115 年 10 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
10 月出口 967.6 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 10.9%，較上
年同月增 58.9%(按新臺幣計算增 66.6%)；累 計 1 至 10 月出口 7,582.9
億美元，較上年同期增 47.7%(按新臺幣計算增 51.1%)。 
（二）進口 
10 月進口 688.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 8.3%，較上
年同月增 75.1%(按新臺幣計算增 83.5%)；累 計 1 至 10 月進口 5,697.6
億美元，較上年同期增 44.6%(按新臺幣計算增 47.6%)。 
（三）出入超 
10 月出超 278.8 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 63.3 億美元；
累計 1 至 10 月出超 1,885.4 億美元，較上年同期增 689.7 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續拓展，
加上適逢傳統外銷旺季，以及價格攀升效應，10 月出口較上年同月
增 358.7 億美元(+58.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 476.0 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增
200.7 億美元(+72.9%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增 151.6 億美元
(+67.5%)、電腦之零附件增 18.5 億美元(+1.0 倍)、儲存媒體增 17.3
億美元(+2.1 倍)、交換器及路由器增 11.7 億美元(+1.3 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 326.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 124.3
億美元(+61.4%)，其中積體電路增 120.5 億美元(+63.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.9 億美元(+38.5%)、電容器及電阻器增 1.2 億美元(+48.6%)。 
3. 基本金屬及其製品出口 28.8 億美元，較上年同月增 7.6 億美元
(+35.7%)，其中銅及其製品增 4.7 億美元(+1.2 倍)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.9 億美元(+16.4%)。 
4. 機械出口 28.8 億美元(歷年單月新高)，較上年同月增 7.5 億美元
(+35.5%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.9 億美元(+57.5%)、機器、
工廠或實驗室設備增 1.4 億美元(+70.0%)。 
5. 礦產品出口 15.5 億美元，較上年同月增 6.5 億美元(+71.6%)，其
中石油煉製品增 5.7 億美元(+72.2%)。

（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月出口較上年同期增 2,448.3 億美元(+47.7%)，以資通
與視聽產品增 1,423.3 億美元(+72.5%)、電子零組件增 850.5 億美元
(+47.5%)增加較多，礦產品、機械亦各增 41.9 億美元(+36.8%)、41.5
億美元(+19.8%)。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路、資通產品
及半導體設備進口持續強勁，加上國際原物料行情居高，10 月總進
口較上年同月增 295.5 億美元(+75.1%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口 458.3 億美元，較上年同月增 195.0 億美元(+74.0%)，
其中電子零組件增 136.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)、礦產品增 22.8 億美元
(+58.0%)、電腦之零附件增 16.2 億美元(+70.0%)、基本金屬增 6.2
億美元(+33.4%)、塑化原料增 5.0 億美元(+22.0%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 163.4 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通設備增 46.9 億美元(+2.0 倍)、半導體設備增 22.4 億美元
(+83.4%)、交通運輸設備增 7.6 億美元(+7.0 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 61.1 億美元，較上年同月增 18.8 億美元(+44.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.0 億美元(+5.3 倍)、食品增 0.5 億
美元(+5.1%)、小客車增 0.4 億美元(+10.2%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月對美國、歐洲、日本出口創新高 
1. 對美國出口 329.3 億美元，較上年同月增 118.1 億美元(+55.9%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 98.3 億美元(+56.6%)、電子零組件增 13.5
億美元(+1.7 倍)、機械增 2.4 億美元(+41.6%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 204.4 億美元，較上年同月增 61.4 億美元
(+42.9%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+48.0%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.6 億美元(+36.5%)、基本金屬及其製品增 3.2 億美元
(+95.2%)。 
3. 對東協出口 184.6 億美元，較上年同月增 84.1 億美元(+83.8%)，
其中電子零組件增 38.6 億美元(+75.5%)、資通與視聽產品增 36.5
億美元(+1.4 倍)、礦產品增 5.0 億美元(+2.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 59.5 億美元，較上年同月增 18.7 億美元(+46.0%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 10.7 億美元(+47.9%)、電子零組件增 3.3 億
美元(+81.3%)、機械增 1.2 億美元(+50.8%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.7 億美元，較上年同月增 12.2 億美元(+49.6%)，其
中電子零組件增 7.5 億美元(+63.2%)、資通與視聽產品增 3.2 億美
元(+1.3 倍)、化學品增 0.5 億美元(+47.0%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月對美國出口 2,407.1 億美元，較上年同期增 891.1 億美
元(+58.8%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,805.3 億美元，增 416.6 億美
元(+30.0%)，對東協出口 1,524.1 億美元，增 547.6 億美元(+56.1%)，
對歐洲出口 481.3 億美元，增 157.1 億美元(+48.5%)，對日本出口 314.4
億美元，增 70.5 億美元(+28.9%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
10 月自東協進口 134.1 億美元，較上年同月增 82.3 億美元(+1.6 倍)，
自南韓進口 130.0 億 美 元，增 73.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)，自中國大陸與香
港進口 128.3 億美元，增 51.9 億美元(+67.9%)，自日本進口 62.0 億
美元，增 18.8 億美元(+43.4%)，自美國進口 53.6 億美元，增 14.7 億
美元(+37.9%)，自歐洲進口 48.2 億美元，增 9.5 億美元(+24.6%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 10 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較，自東協增 85.6%、
自南韓增 72.7%、自中國大陸與香港增 45.3%、自日本增 27.4%、自
美國增 23.0%、自歐洲增 12.1%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-9 月主要國家/地區出口均呈上升，其中南韓(1-10 月)、香港、
新加坡受 AI 相關需求帶動科技供應鏈及區域貿易活動熱絡，累計出
口分別年增 60.6%、52.3%、41.3%；中國大陸、美國各年增 21.3%
及 14.3%，德國(1-8 月)、日本各增 8.8%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步成長，累計 1-9 月以香港進口年增 51.8%較
高，新加坡、中國大陸、南 韓 (1-10 月)分別年增 39.0%、27.5%及 21.4%，
德國(1-8 月)、日本、美國各增 8.4%、8.2%、6.7%。

### Page 7

7
圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）隨全球人工智慧(AI)基礎建設投資強勁成長，帶動相關供應鏈需求擴
增，加以外銷產品價格攀升，10 月出口達 967.6 億美元，首度突破
900 億美元，年增 58.9%，連續 36 個月正成長；進口 688.9 億美元，
同為歷年單月最高，年增 75.1%，主要受惠於 AI 產業之國際分工與
出口引申需求增加，以及國際原物料行情高漲；出超 278.8 億美元亦
創新高。累計 1 至 10 月出口年增 47.7%、進口增 44.6%。
（二）10 月出口主要貨品中，資通與視聽產品、電子零組件出口值雙創單月
新高，各年增 72.9%及 61.4%，主因 AI、高效能運算及雲端服務等應
用商機熱絡；其餘貨類出口平均增 25.7%，其中礦產品因油價支撐增
71.6%，機械、電機產品受惠於半導體業者擴充產能及 AI 需求外溢，
增幅皆達 3 成，基本金屬及其製品亦增 35.7%。累 計 1 至 10 月資通與
視聽產品、電子零組件合占總出口比重約 8 成，年增 60.6%。

（三）10 月對主要市場出口年增率均逾 4 成，以對東協增 83.8%較突出，
對美國、日本、歐洲出口規模皆為單月新高，各增 55.9%、49.6%及
46.0%，對中國大陸與香港增 42.9%。累 計 1 至 10 月對五大市場出口
增幅介於 28%~59%，其中對美國出口年增 58.8%、對東協增 56.1%，
占總出口比重各為 31.7%及 20.1%，分別寫下近 36 年同期及歷年同
期新高紀錄。
（四）展望未來，全球經濟仍面臨地緣政治衝突、美國貿易政策走向等不
確定因素，惟隨人工智慧應用逐漸擴散至推論、代理式 AI，且國際
雲端服務業者持續布建 AI 基礎設施，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供
應鏈需求延續，加以科技新品相繼推 出、年底消費旺季來臨之挹注，
我國第 4 季出口可望維持強勁成長態勢。

2026. 11.10
先週（11/2〜11/9）発表の主な経済指標
⽶国雇⽤は安定も、AI曝露度の⾼い職業で若年雇⽤が弱含
み。雇⽤不安が⺠主党躍進を後押しした可能性。AI関連株が
調整するなか、韓国中銀は投資減速のアジアへの波及を警戒
所定内給与は物価上昇率を上回る伸びが持続。冬季ボーナス
も押し上げ。電気・ガス料⾦補助再開は消費の追い⾵に。⼀⽅、
消費回復が進めば基調的な物価上昇圧⼒を強める可能性
注目点② 賃上げの波及と物価対策が日本の消費を下支え注目点① 米AI投資拡大の裏で広がる「不安」と「リスク」
⾮農業部⾨雇⽤者数は市場予想を上回り、失業
率も低下。ISM景況指数は製造業・⾮製造業とも
に拡⼤圏を維持。雇⽤・企業活動の底堅さの⼀⽅
で、消費者マインドは持ち直すも依然として低⽔準

失業率
米国：雇用統計は良好な結果。一方で、賃金上昇もインフレ見合いで見劣り
 10月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋16.5万人と市場予想（同＋6.5万人）を大きく上回る結果
―過去2カ月も合わせて8.4万人の上方修正。業種別では専門ビジネスサービスやヘルスケアが全体をけん引
― 時間当たり賃金も前月比＋0.4％（前年比＋3.1％）と着実に増加。ただしインフレ率に割り負けている状況が継続
 失業率は4.1％と前月（4.2％）から小幅低下も、失業期間は長期化
― 失業期間の中央値は11.9週（9月：同11.5週）と長期化。27週以上の長期失業者も増加基調維持。企業の採用率は低下

傾向が続いており、労働者が失業状態から脱しにくい傾向が継続
3

（年）
米国：AI普及の影響は若年層に偏在。雇用機会への不安が拡大
 AI曝露度別の雇用指数をみると、22～34歳では曝露度の高い職業の雇用が相対的に弱含み
― AIの影響がキャリア形成段階の若年層に偏っている可能性
 若年層では、AIによる雇用代替に加え、物価高など生活面の不安も根強い状況
― AIを否定的に捉える割合も若い世代ほど高く、AI普及への警戒感がうかがえる状況
4
（注） 上図の若年層とは18～29歳。経済状況は回答者の主観的な評価

（出所） Harvard Youth Poll、ABC News、Washington Post、Ipsosより、みずほ総合研
究所調査部作成
AI曝露度別雇用指数
年齢別AIの認識：「AIは社会に悪い」と回答した割合
（注） 1．AI曝露度とは各職業の業務が生成AIの影響を受ける度合いを示す。職業を曝露度
に応じて5分位に分類し、低・中・高はそれぞれ第1・第3・第5分位を指す
2．年齢層別の雇用指数は、米国雇用統計の家計調査における年齢別雇用者数をウェ
イトに加重平均して算出。年齢区分が一致しない部分は、各階級内の雇用者数が均
等に分布すると仮定して按分
（出所）スタンフォード大学デジタル経済研究所、ADP Researchより、みずほ総合研究所調査
部作成

米国：若年層の経済的不安が中間選挙の投票行動にも影響か
 2026年中間選挙では「物価高」や「雇用」への不安が若年層の主要な関心事項に
― こうした経済的不安が共和党政権への不満につながった可能性
― 年齢別の投票動向では、若年層を中心に民主党支持が優勢。生活費負担や将来の雇用機会への不安が投票行動に
影響した模様
― なお、議会でも世代交代が進展。若い世代の政策選好が今後の政策形成に与える影響にも注目

### Page 7

（参考）民主党が掲げる主な政策
 勝利した民主党は、若年層の不満とも重なる生活費負担の軽減（アフォーダビリティ）を共通の政策課題に位置付け
 具体的な政策手段を巡っては、中道派と左派・進歩派の間で違い
― 中道派は競争促進や規制緩和、財政規律を重視する一方、左派・進歩派は大企業への規制・課税強化や政府支出の
拡大を主張。今後の党内調整が焦点に
6

（年）
（2020＝100）
アジア：AI投資減速の影響が域内の貿易ネットワークを通じて波及するリスク
 韓国中銀はAI関連投資の拡大に伴う過剰投資やレバレッジの高まり、循環金融などの金融リスクを警戒
― AI産業の期待収益の過大評価や最終的なリスク所在把握が困難になり、リスクが過小評価される可能性があると指摘
― また、米国企業のAI投資が減速する悲観シナリオでは、韓国の半導体輸出が大幅に減少し、GDP成長率を0.4％Pt押し
下げると試算
― 韓国のAI関連財貿易は中国・台湾との取引が4割超。アジア域内で形成された貿易ネットワークを通じて、AI投資減速の
影響が広範に波及する可能性

日本：物価対策の再開が家計負担を軽減。消費の回復を後押し
9
 政府は冬場の電気・ガス料金補助の再開を決定。標準的な家庭で計7,000円程度の負担軽減を見込む
― 一方、ガソリン価格の補助は段階的に縮小（基準額を毎月5円ずつ引き上げ）。電気・ガス料金補助金の効果を一部減殺
 補助金を巡る政策変更は、コアCPIの前年比を最大0.4％程度押し下げ
― 賃金上昇に加え、政策による物価負担軽減が家計の実質購買力を下支え
― 物価対策がCPIを押し下げる一方、消費回復を通じて基調的な物価上昇圧力が強まる可能性には留意が必要

（注） ガソリンと同額の補助を実施している軽油・重油・灯油、ガソリン補助額の4割相当
の支援を実施している航空機燃料については未定

欧州：10月消費者物価はエネルギー主導で加速。企業は販売価格の引き上げを計画
10

― 一方、コアは同＋2.5％と前月から不変。エネルギー高の二次的な影響はいまだ顕在化せず

中国：政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更。銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索
 中国人民銀行は政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更（11/2）。短期市場金利の誘導精度を高める狙い
― リバースレポ7日物から切り替え、翌日物のオペを毎営業日実施。最優遇貸出金利（LPR）も翌日物金利を参照へ
― インターバンク市場における債券担保付レポ翌日物金利（DR001）が誘導目標と明言。短期金利の安定化を図る考え
 銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索。DR001を参照して貸出金利を決定する方法を試験的に導入

― 背景には、銀行貸出の指標金利であるLPRが「最優遇」から乖離し、LPRより低利での貸出比率が上昇している実態
― 今後、財務力のある企業は銀行と協議の上でDR001かLPRを選択し、小規模企業や個人には引き続きLPRを適用へ

中国：景況感は再び悪化し、2カ月ぶりの節目割れ。製造業は需給ともに振るわず
 10月の景況感は再び悪化。総合PMIは49.7と前月（50.7）から大幅に低下し、2カ月ぶりの節目割れ
― うち非製造業PMIが49.7と前月（50.2）から低下も、宿泊やスポーツ・娯楽が持ち直し、情報サービスは堅調だった
― 建設業PMIは48.0と前月（50.3）から急低下。中国物流購買聯合会は建設工事の着工が遅れ気味と指摘
 製造業PMIは49.3と前月（50.1）から低下。供給側の生産、需要側の受注がともに振るわず

― 生産が49.8（前月：51.7）、新規受注が49.4（同：50.5）、新規輸出受注も48.7（同：50.0）といずれも節目の50を下回る
― 中東情勢に伴う価格上昇に一服感。原材料価格は56.8（同：60.8）、出荷価格は51.7（同：54.0）まで戻す

中国：PPIは生産財が急上昇も、川下への価格転嫁進まず。CPIは食品価格が下押し要因に
 10月のPPIは前年比＋5.7％（前月：同＋5.0％）。中東情勢の影響による資源・エネルギー関連業種の価格上昇続く
― 石油・石炭加工（同＋29.9％）と非鉄金属加工（同＋18.6％）、化学品（同＋16.4％）が引き続き高い伸び
― 川上の生産財が同＋7.4％まで上昇も、川下の消費財は同▲0.1％と下落継続。価格転嫁は一向に進まず
 CPIは同＋0.7％と、前月（同＋0.9％）から減速。食品価格の下落（▲2.9％）が下押し要因に（▲0.49％Pt）

― 他方、燃料価格高騰により、ガソリンは同＋22.4％（前月：同＋16.6％）まで上昇し、全体を＋0.63％Pt押し上げ
― 食品・エネルギーを除くコアは同＋0.8％（前月：＋1.0％）と弱いが、通信機器はメモリー高騰で同＋16.1％の伸び

### Page 15

アジア：ASEAN5の製造業景況感は概ね堅調。原油高など仕入コスト上昇の影響は残存
 ASEAN5の10月の製造業景況感は、フィリピンを除き改善圏。受注・生産は好調も、コスト高が継続
― タイ、ベトナムは高水準で推移し、インドネシアは回復が鮮明。一方、フィリピンが基準となる50を下回る水準
― S&Pはリリース資料において、新規受注の増加が生産を支えたものの、仕入コスト上昇と原料調達・物流の制約を指摘
 ASEAN5では、生産者物価（PPI）が高止まり。原油高の影響が残存
― 原油高が続く中、石炭・石油に加えて化学やプラ・ゴム等の製品価格にも上昇圧力が継続
― 仕入コストの高止まりが、企業収益や生産活動の重荷となるリスクに留意が必要

### Page 16

 ASEAN5の一部の国で財政収支が悪化
― ベトナム、マレーシア、インドネシアで改善した一方で、フィリピンとタイで財政赤字が拡大し、明暗が分かれる結果に
― フィリピンは税収の伸び悩み、タイは景気下支えに向けた公共投資拡大や予算執行の加速などにより歳出が大きく増加
 各国とも財政面の制約を抱え、追加的な景気下支え余地は限定的
― タイは公的債務残高が法定上限（GDP比70％）に接近。マレーシアも政府債務残高が高水準
― フィリピンは財政赤字の縮小が課題。インドネシアは財政赤字上限（GDP比3％）が制約要因

## Document 088: 2026年7-9月期GDP（１次速報値） ～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7-9月期GDP（１次速報値） ～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Indicators

2026年 11月16日

1/5 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（１次速報値） 
～一時的な下押しで足踏みも、10-12月期は再び持ち直しへ～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

小幅マイナス成長も、景気回復の基調は維持 
本日内閣府から公表された 2026年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率（１次速報）は前期比年率
▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と、４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった1。個人消費が前期比▲0.3％
と減少したほか、住宅投資や公共投資も減少し、成長率を押し下げている。一方、設備投資は前
期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えした。概ね事前の市場予想（前期比年率＋0.2％、
筆者予想：同▲0.4％）の範囲内であり、サプライズはない。 
今回減少した個人消費については、過度に悲観する必要はないだろう。４-６月期にみられた自
動車やエアコンなどの耐久財需要の一時的な押し上げが一巡したことに加え、７-９月期には台風
や大雨、長雨などの天候不順が相次いだことも下押し要因となったとみられる。一方、名目賃金
の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、家計の所得環境は足元で改善している。食料品
や日用品などを中心とした物価の高止まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、今回の減
少をもって個人消費の基調が悪化したと判断する必要はないだろう。 
一方、設備投資が高い伸びとなったことは好材料だ。今回の増加には前期の特殊要因の反動も
含まれているが、それを除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持す
るなか、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。企業
部門の底堅さが改めて確認されたと評価できる。 
このように、７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰは４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となったが、減少幅は僅か
だ。前期まで３四半期連続で比較的高い成長が続いていたことに加え、今回の個人消費の減少に
は、耐久財需要の剥落や８～９月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因が影響したとみられる
点も重要だ。今回のマイナス成長をもって景気回復の流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろ
う。家計部門は力強さを欠く一方、設備投資を中心に企業部門は堅調さを維持している。家計と
企業の回復に濃淡はみられるものの、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると評価で
きる。

1 ＧＤＰ統計では、季節調整のかけ直しや基礎統計の改定に伴い過去の値も毎回改定される。25年４-６月期が前期比
年率＋0.8％（改定前＋0.5％）、７-９月期が▲1.5％（同▲1.5％）、10-12月期が＋1.1％（同＋1.1％）、26年１-３月期
が＋1.9％（同＋1.9％）、４-６月期が＋1.7％（同＋1.4％）などとなった。符号の変化等はみられず、景気認識に変更
を迫るほどではなかった。

### Page 2

2/5

輸入増による成長率押し下げと公的在庫のプラス寄与は表裏一体 
今回のＧＤＰを評価する際に注意が必要なのは、輸入と公的在庫の動きである。 
４-６月期は、中東情勢の悪化によって原油輸入が激減したことが、ＧＤＰ統計上は輸入減を通
じて成長率を押し上げた。一方、輸入の減少による国内供給の不足を補うために国家備蓄原油が
放出され、公的在庫の取り崩しが進んだ。この結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度▲0.5％Pt（前
期比年率寄与度▲2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大きく押し下げた。輸入減による成長率の押し上げは、
公的在庫の減少と表裏一体の関係にあった。 
７-９月期は、これと逆の動きが生じた。政府・民間による代替調達の進展によって原油輸入が
回復したため、統計上は輸入増が成長率の押し下げ要因となった。一方、輸入の回復に伴って国
家備蓄原油の取り崩しは一服しており、公的在庫の減少幅は前期に比べて大きく縮小した。この
結果、公的在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.5％Pt（前期比年率寄与度＋2.0％Pt）と、成長率を大き
く押し上げている。 
輸入増による成長率の押し下げ（前期比年率寄与度▲2.2％Pt）と公的在庫変動による押し上げ
（同寄与度＋2.0％Pt）は、ともに原油の供給環境が改善したことに伴う動きである。両者を個別
に景気の強弱として評価することは適切ではなく、一体として見るべきだろう。 
むしろ重要なのは、代替調達の進展によって国家備蓄への依存を減らしながら必要な原油を確
保できる状況になり、資源不足が生産活動を大きく制約する事態が回避されたことである。ＧＤ
Ｐ統計上、輸入の増加は成長率の押し下げ要因となるものの、経済実態としては国内で利用可能
な資源の供給回復を意味している。この点は、７-９月期の日本経済を評価するうえで、むしろ前
向きに捉えるべきだろう。

10-12月期はプラス成長を予想 
 先行きについても、景気は緩やかな回復基調を維持すると予想する。７-９月期は４四半期ぶり
のマイナス成長となったが、耐久財特需の剥落や天候不順による一時的な下押しが和らぐことか
ら、10-12月期には再びプラス成長に戻る可能性が高い。名目賃金の高い伸びや雇用の改善が続く
なか、所得環境の改善が個人消費を下支えするとみられる。 
ただし、今後は物価上昇率の再加速が個人消費の重荷になると予想される。既往の原油高や原
材料費、物流費などの上昇が時間差を伴って川下へ波及しつつある。今後は食料品や日用品など
への価格転嫁がさらに進むことに加え、電気・ガス代も上昇するだろう。物価上昇率の加速によ
り、足元で続いている実質賃金の改善が一服する恐れがある。個人消費は先行き持ち直すとみる
が、回復ペースは力強さを欠くとみられる。 
一方、企業部門については今後も底堅い推移が期待できる。企業収益が高水準を維持するな
か、人手不足に対応した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は根強い。輸入資本
財価格や建設コストの上昇は下押し要因となるものの、設備投資は緩やかな増加基調を続けると
予想する。また、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さやグローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大も、輸

### Page 3

3/5 
出や生産を下支えするだろう。 
このように、10-12月期は個人消費が緩やかに持ち直すことに加え、設備投資や輸出の増加が景
気を下支えし、再びプラス成長になると予想する。物価高による個人消費への下押しには注意が
必要だが、企業部門の堅調さが維持されることで、日本経済は緩やかな回復を続けるだろう。

需要項目別の詳細 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と２四半期連続で減少した。形態別では、耐久財消費が前期比▲
3.5％（４-６月期：同＋3.6％）、非耐久財消費が同▲0.2％（４-６月期：同▲1.9％）、半耐久財
消費が同▲1.4％（４-６月期：同＋0.4％）、サービス消費が同＋0.1％（４-６月期：同▲0.1％）
となっている。４-６月期には環境性能割の廃止を受けた自動車販売の増加や、27年４月の省エネ
基準強化を控えたエアコンの前倒し購入など、耐久財消費の特需がみられたが、７-９月期にはこ
うした押し上げ効果が一巡した。また、８～９月には記録的な豪雨や台風、長雨などの天候不順
が相次いだことが、外出手控えによる消費機会の減少や旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて下押し
に繋がったとみられる。また、非耐久財消費にも弱さがみられる。食料品や日用品の価格上昇が
続くなか、家計の節約志向が根強いことに加え、天候不順による季節商品の販売減少なども下押
しに効いた可能性がある。 
もっとも、名目賃金の高い伸びを背景に実質賃金の増加が続くなど、所得環境は足元で改善し
ており、家計を取り巻く環境が悪化しているわけではない。食料品を中心とした価格水準の高止
まりが消費の回復を妨げている面はあるものの、４-６月期には高校授業料の実質無償化拡充や学
校給食費無償化に伴う個人消費から政府消費への振り替わりなどの特殊要因があり、今回７-９月
期についても耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが大きく影響したとみられ
る。個人消費が力強さを欠いていることは確かだが、今回の減少をもって消費の基調が悪化した
と判断する必要はないだろう。 
先行きについては、耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な下押しが和らぐことに加
え、賃金上昇や雇用の改善が下支えとなることから、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直すと予想する。
ただし、26年度後半には既往の輸入コスト上昇の価格転嫁が進み、食料品や日用品などの値上が
りや電気・ガス代の上昇が家計の実質購買力を圧迫する可能性が高い。物価上昇率の再加速によ
って実質賃金の改善が一服するリスクがあり、消費の回復ペースは当面緩やかなものにとどまる
だろう。 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と高い伸びとなり、３四半期ぶりに増加した。４-６月期には特許権
の海外譲渡に伴う研究開発投資の減少という特殊要因により押し下げられていたが、７-９月期に
はその影響が剥落したことが前期比での押し上げ要因となった。もっとも、こうした特殊要因を
除いても設備投資は増加したと考えられる。企業収益が高水準を維持するなか、人手不足に対応
した省力化投資やデジタル・ＡＩ関連投資などへの需要は引き続き強い。各種調査でも企業の設
備投資計画は高い伸びを維持しており、設備投資は均してみれば緩やかな増加基調にあると判断
できる。

### Page 4

4/5
先行きについても、輸入資本財価格や建設コストの上昇が設備投資の下押し要因となるもの
の、高水準の企業収益や根強い省力化・デジタル・ＡＩ関連投資需要が下支えとなり、緩やかな
増加基調が続くと予想する。
なお、特許権の海外への譲渡により、テクニカルに４-６月期はサービス輸出が上振れ、設備投
資が下振れたが、７-９月期はその剥落によりサービス輸出が下振れ、設備投資が上振れている。
両者は相殺し合うため、成長率全体でみれば概ねニュートラルとなる。
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と小幅減となった。４-６月期には大口要因によって研究開発サービスの
受取が急増していたが、７-９月期にはその反動によってサービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（４-６月
期：＋8.9％）と大幅に減少した。一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（４-６月期：▲0.8％）と持ち
直している。グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、半導体製造装置などの資本財輸出が好
調だったほか、４-６月期に大きく落ち込んだ中東向け輸出に回復の動きがみられたことも財輸出

の押し上げ要因になった。サービス輸出の特殊要因による振れを除けば、海外景気の底堅さとＡ
Ｉ関連需要の拡大を背景に、輸出は緩やかな増加基調を維持していると評価できる。
先行きについても、米国を中心とした海外景気の底堅さに加え、グローバルなＡＩ関連需要の
拡大が半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出を下支えすることから、緩やかな増加基調が続くと
予想する。
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と増加した。４-６月期には資源輸入の激減により輸入が減少していた
が、代替調達の進展により７-９月期は明確に持ち直した。この結果、外需寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％Pt（前期比年率寄与度▲2.4％Pt）と大幅なマイナスになった。
ただし、輸入増加による成長率の押し下げは、原油などの供給環境が改善したことに伴うもの
であり、需要の弱さを示すものではない。また、輸入増加の裏側では、国家備蓄原油の取り崩し
が一服したことに伴って公的在庫変動が前期比で＋0.5％ポイント、前期比年率では＋2.0％ポイ
ントの大幅プラス寄与となっている。両者は一体として評価する必要があるだろう。

民間在庫変動は前期比寄与度＋0.0％Ptと、成長率にはほぼニュートラルだった。製品在庫と原
材料在庫が押し上げに寄与した一方、流通在庫と仕掛品在庫が下押し要因になった。
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と、２四半期連続で減少した。建設コストの上昇が下押し要因となっ
ており、住宅投資を取り巻く環境は引き続き厳しい。先行きについても、建設コストの高止まり
や金利上昇の影響から、低調な推移が続く可能性が高い。
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と概ね横ばいとなった。４-６月期に前期比＋1.5％と高い伸びとなっ
た後、増勢が一服している。もっとも、医療・介護関連の支出などは趨勢的に増加しており、政
府消費の弱さは前期の反動による一時的なものと考えられる。
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と減少した。政府の国土強靱化関連工事などが下支えにはなっている
が、建設コストの上昇が実質公共投資の下押し要因になっているとみられる。

### Page 5

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
5/5

（出所）内閣府「国民経済計算」

新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，

 投資評價與建議 
維持買進，目標價降至 358 元：(1) 3Q26 獲利表現不如預期，主因 PCB 比重拉升
及面對中國在地價格競爭，(2) 能見度高，但隨企業 AI Server 帶動 HDI/HLC PCB 
與 ABF 載板 2H26-2027 年接力放量，短期獲利改善有限。我們預估 2026/ 2027
年 EPS 為 14.01/19.92 元，目標 PE 則由 30x 下修至 18x，反映市場對設備類股評
價的調整，目標價隨之下修到 358 元(18x 2027 EPSF)。

### Page 2

2

志聖以光與熱為核心，發展熱處理平台：志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製
程等技術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備
起家，而後伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、面板、太陽能等產業，近
年陸續在 IC 載板、高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值市場發光發熱。公司
也與均豪、均華、東捷組成 G2C+聯盟，共同實現台灣先進封裝在地化。志聖主要
生產據點位於台灣林口、台中、中國廣州、昆山，並在台南、廣東、泰國、美國鳳
凰城有服務據點。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、
傳統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。主要競爭對手包含 EVG、TOK、SUSS、
Yield Engineering Systems(YES)等。客戶包含台積電、日月光、臻鼎、力成、
Amkor、Micron、欣興、勝宏等電子中上游廠商。 
3Q26 獲利不如預期：志聖 3Q26 營收 29.8 億(+7.0%QoQ，+93.9%YoY)，毛利
率 43.3%，稅後淨利 5.5 億(-13.4%QoQ，+162.1%YoY)，EPS 3.51 元。毛利率
表現不如預期主因出貨較多中國 PCB 用低階設備，使產品組合轉差，即半導體設
備/PCB 設備由 1H26 的 46%/46%變動至 1-3Q26 的 45%/49%，營業費用亦隨
獎酬增加及研發專案數的提升而高於預期。 
能見度高，但我們對獲利趨於審慎：管理層預期 2026 年底在手訂單約 100-110
億，較去年同期約 43 億元倍增，其中半導體佔比約 53%以上(以 OSAT 能見度較
高)、高階 PCB 35-37%。值得注意的是，半導體設備交期已由 4-10 個月拉長至 4-
13 個月；PCB 設備交期則約 3-8 個月，主因 OSAT 在廠房未完工前即下單，以及
上游零組件(機械手臂)交期的拉長所致，志聖持續以 G2C+聯盟和日系供應商溝通
並聯合採購，目前風險可控。展望後市，我們預期在晶圓廠先進封裝產能建置外溢
的環境下，志聖將透過挑單生產，降低傳統 PCB 接單比例以維持獲利率穩健成長。 
載板挖槽嵌入製程，2028 年再添動能：隨 AI 晶片尺寸持續放大，台系載板廠正
發展新的生產方式，以降低整片翹曲同時達到微縮線寬/線距的目的。志聖在此製
程中提供背面貼膜，以及 ABF 絕緣膜的壓合與撕膜設備。由於層數倍增將使壓合
與撕膜次數同步增加，加上載具尺寸放大推升單機價值，我們認為這將是志聖 
2028 年後載板設備的重要成長來源。 
財務預估：研究部預估志聖 2026 年營收 111.1 億元(+82.2%YoY)，毛利率 46.3%，
稅後淨利 22.7 億元(+173.0%YoY)，EPS 14.01 元(原估 16.94 元，下修主因本業
獲利表現不如預期及股本膨脹)。展望 2027 年，公司尚未給予明確展望，我們考量
短期 PCB/HLC 急單湧現壓抑毛利率，故下修獲利預估，預期 2027 年營收 148.4
億元(+33.5%YoY)，毛利率 46.9%，稅後淨利 32.2 億元(+42.2%YoY)，EPS 19.92
元(原估 25.15 元，下修主因獲利預估調降及股本膨脹)。

## Document 153: 261117_ml_ABF-CCL

Source PDF: all report pdf/261117_ml_ABF-CCL.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 20
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Technology - Asia-Pacific (H)

ABF substrate & CCL: making the case for 
limited downside after valuation reset

Price Objective Change

Valuation implies sizable 2028 EPS downside   
Broadly speaking, share prices of the ABF substrate and CCL stocks under our Greater 
China coverage fell by 40-50% from their October peaks to their November lows. Based 
on the two scenarios presented in this report, we believe current valuations may imply 
the market’s over-concerns about the inflection of AI server cycle or a slowdown in 
capex growth. Using closing price as of Nov. 16th, the stocks traded at 7-13x 2028E P/E 
under scenario 1, in which we assume a 15% shortfall in AI-related revenue vs. our 
estimates (Exhibit 3). These multiples are below the midpoint of their valuation range 
before the rise of AI server demand from mid-2023. Under scenario 2, we divide the 
current share price by our long-term anchored P/E multiple to calculate the 2028E EPS 
imlied by the share price. Based on our analysis, current valuations imply a 40-90% 
shortfall (or discount) in AI-related revenue versus our estimates (Exhibit 4). 
Raise estimates but lower P/E multiples  
For 2027-28E OP, we raise our estimates by 4-9%/19-31%/3-6%/19-24%/22-23%/32-
33% for Unimicron/Kinsus/NYPCB/EMC/TUC/Shengyi, reflecting a general beat in 3Q26 
GM and ongoing price hike outlook across ABF/BT substrate and CCL. However, we 
lower our POs on these names (Exhibit 1) as we lower P/E multiples by ~30% to reflect 
sector-wide de-rating in AI server supply chain, while the valuation period is unchanged. 
See Exhibit 10 for details of the changes in valuation multiples.  
Stay largely positive on ABF substrate and CCL sectors 
Despite lowered POs and multiples, our positive stance on the ABF substrate sector is 
unchanged, reflecting our expectation of a more sustained supply deficit through 2028 
as well as the T-glass price hike by Nittobo (see report) and Resonac, which should set 
the stage for substrate makers to lift ASP further. We thus reiterate Buy on Unimicron, 
Kinsus and NYPCB. As for CCL, we are also generally positive on the S/D outlook given 
the lead time of certain equipment is still at least 1.5-2 years, yet at single stock level 
we are more constructive on Buy-rated names including 1) EMC with at least 70-80% 
revenue mix from M7-grade and above in 2027-28 and its estimated 50-60% share of 
the AI-related high-end CCL market, along with potential exposure to M10 adoption in 
SpaceX’s AI server rack based on our channel checks; and 2) TUC being one of the few 
high-end CCL suppliers with more aggressive capacity expansion during 2026-30 (~3x 
over the period). We also see potential for additional ~2mn sheet/month capacity 
addition in 2030 (in Thailand).   For discussions on ABF substrate S/D model update and 
our thoughts on CPO impact, see page 2.

### Page 2

ABF substrate – wider undersupply expected vs previously  
We update ABF substrate industry S/D model with this report, and we now expect 
7%/18%/20% under-supply (was 7%/14%/19%) in 2026/27/28 thanks to the raised TAM 
(see report, Core Wars: raising CPU TAM to $240bn, still early stages of agents) for AI 
accelerators and CPUs (central processing units), which leads us to estimate 36%/24% 
(was 32%/26%) demand growth by the industry in 2027/28. After reflecting the demand 
updates, AI accelerators are expected to account for 41%/52%/55% (was 
40%/46%/51%) of demand in 2026/27/28. Overall, our industry S/D model also 
considers the 2.5x SAP (semi additive process) demand growth across 2026-30 shared 
by Ibiden, which incorporates 1.5x and 4.4x demand growth by server CPU and AI 
accelerators respectively over the same period.  
Thoughts on CPO impact for CCL/PCB 
Our checks with supply chain suggest that many of the CCL materials are upgraded to 
M9-grade (based on low Dk2 cloth and hyper very low profile 4 copper foil), when 
pluggable optical modules are upgraded to OBO (on-board optics) and/or NPO (near 
package optics). In terms of CCL-grade vs PCB layer count, there is a general 
categorization in AI server and adjacent applications shown in Exhibit 12, and it indicates 
that M8-grade or above CCL will be required when the board layer count moves up to 30 
or higher. Further, given that CPO gen-2 with OOI (optics on interposer) is unlikely to 
ramp before 2028, alongside the upgrade in transmission to 3.2T, CCL material used in 
mainboard may have already been upgraded to M10-grade by then under a non- 
CPO/OOI architecture. This means CPO/OOI adoption may only result in a material 
downgrade to M8/M9-grade.

Price objective basis & risk 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$8,200 is based on 30x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the mid-to-high-end of its 
historical range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the 
market-share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in 
addressable market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

### Page 13

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kinsus (KNSUF) 
We set our PO at NT$1,450 based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at mid-to-high-end 
level in its historical trading range (8-40x) since 2013. In our view, the multiple is backed 
by ROE and operating margin recovery to 36% and 25% in 2027-28E (vs 8% and 10% in 
2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) increasing adoption of SiP and AiP, 
(3) lower competition pressure and (4) slow development of alternative technology.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, (3) 
more severe competition, and (4) unfavorable technology development. 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the mid-to-high-end of 
the historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is justified, given operating margin and ROE expansion toward 26% and 63% in 
2026-28E (vs 13% and 16% in 2023-25) along with robust free-cash flow generation in 
over the same period.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers 
Unimicron (UMCRF) 
Our PO of NT$1,650 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E, which is roughly 1 standard deviation 
above the historical average of 20x since 2017. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, 
as we expect Unimicron's earnings to be supported by its high-end technology offerings, 
where end market demand seems more resilient.

Upside risks are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) greater PC and regular server demand, and (3) 
lower competition pressure.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) lower ASP content increase 
at AI server, and (3) bigger drop in the demand of consumer electronic products.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

Realtek Semiconductor  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Realtek remains relatively resilient despite sluggish PC demand, supported by its
diversified product portfolio and platform agnostic strategy. Near-term customer
inventory adjustments appear to reflect shipment timing rather than underlying
demand weakness. Meanwhile, elevated legacy DRAM prices continue to pressure
device BOM costs, potentially delaying broader adoption of edge Al and other

consumer applications
Looking ahead, edge server and edge Al represent structural growth opportunities,
supported by ongoing development in high-speed SerDes and integrated Al
solutions. Realtek also sees opportunities to strengthen its position in higher-end TV
SoCs amid competition. A normalization in memory prices could provide an
additional catalyst for downstream demand recovery.

12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

기아 (000270) 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결

▶Analyst 김성래 sr.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7751

Buy (유지) 
목표주가(하향): 260,000원

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 YoY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 YoY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지
면서 전년 동기 수익성 악화에 크게 영향을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조지아 텔루라이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스포티지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수요가 높은 북미 현지에서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중심의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추정치는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부정적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것으로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상반기 적용한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목표주가를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상대적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모멘텀에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 297: Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central banks_ a pause while waiting for more information (2026-10-29).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

Central banks: a pause while waiting for more information

As expected, the Fed, the ECB, and the BoJ left their rates unchanged this week. This pause, however, does not mark 
the end of the cycle. The ECB’s tone is becoming more balanced, weighing inflationary risks against tightening financial 
conditions, but one final rate hike in December remains our base case. Across the Atlantic, Kevin Warsh describes an 
environment conducive to further tightening, while remaining cautious about the risk of a credit shock. In Japan, the 
lasting exit from the deflationary cycle is taking shape, and monetary policy adjustment is expected to continue 
gradually. We anticipate two rate hikes by the Fed by January and four by the BoJ by the second half of 2028.

Eurozone: A more balanced tone, but the course remains unchanged. The ECB’s statement marks a shift from September. 
It now explicitly acknowledges both upside risks to inflation (the magnitude and persistence of the energy shock) and downside 
pressures (tightening financial conditions). The statement that inflation would remain “well above target for an extended period” 
no longer appears. In essence, however, there is little change. A rate hike in December remains our base case scenario, widely 
anticipated and implicitly signaled by Christine Lagarde during the Q&A (“If the current situation is not called into question by the 
next set of economic data, the path is clear”). Lagarde presented the rise in sovereign yields as a global phenomenon that is 
tightening financial conditions; regarding the TPI, she stuck to the usual stance: the ECB will intervene if monetary policy 
transmission were to be hampered “by disorderly movements not justified by fundamentals”. By December, the indirect and 
second-round effects of the energy shock will be closely scrutinised. From our perspective, September’s producer prices offer 
clearer signals, but the increase remains limited to a few sectors: energy, electricity-intensive industries and electrical equipment, 
driven by IA1. For now, companies in the Eurozone, particularly in the services sector, appear to be absorbing the shock. The 
European Commission’s survey released this Thursday indicates a rise in the sales price index in October, which remains in line 
with recent months.

United States: The Fed navigates between risks. The FOMC pauses after September’s rate hike and maintains the federal 
funds target at 3.75%-4.0%. However, Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh outlined a landscape conducive to further tightening: stronger 
growth, “rather positive” labor market conditions, and, above all, inflation that is “too high.” As a result, two dissenting votes (out 
of 12) were cast in favor of a 25bps hike. But the committee also appears to fear the adverse consequences of tightening too 
quickly, which is why it is waiting to “accumulate more information.” AI is increasingly at the heart of monetary policymaking. In 
particular, K. Warsh warned against the risks of an abrupt tightening of interest rate conditions, including for the AI investment 
cycle - which triggered a steepening of the yield curve. He also announced that he would soon (on November 16) attend a hearing 
of the “Productivity and Employment” task force to “refine his views” on the implications of AI for the dual mandate. Governor Lisa 
Cook, who voted for a 25bps hike, had noted ahead of the committee meeting “ongoing inflationary pressures” and a risk that they 
might not “subside quickly”. In addition, K. Warsh announced that there would be fewer meetings resulting in rate decisions: 7 in 
2027 and 6 in 2028, compared with 8 today. The change is expected to take effect starting in the fall of 2027. The meetings freed 
up by this change would allow the committee to focus on “strategic deliberations” and long-term issues.

Japan: A status quo, but a tightening cycle set to continue as the economy emerges from deflation. The Bank of Japan 
decided, in line with our forecasts and the consensus, to keep its policy rate target steady at 1.25% at its October 29-30 meeting. 
One dissenting vote called for a rate hike (25bps), contrasting with the two dovish dissenting votes (opposed to a rate hike) at the 
previous meeting. As in the US, the decision is justified by the need to further analyse risks to economic activity, prices, and 
financial conditions. Above all, the central bank’s outlook for economic activity and prices marks a turning point: it explicitly states 
that “core inflation has reached a level generally in line with price stability” and that it is “expected to remain there.” Governor Ueda 
refined this by noting that it is now necessary to “anchor” inflation at this level before declaring the target achieved. Furthermore, 
the emphasis on “upside risks to prices” and the fact that any rise in core inflation is now deemed “undesirable” indicate that 
monetary tightening will continue.

The monetary tightening cycle continues. The next steps are likely to be more significant in the United States, where inflation 
has exceeded the target for nearly six years and coexists with a favorable outlook for growth and employment. In the Eurozone, 
the rise in inflation is primarily due to a supply shock in energy costs (though it is also fueled by demand in the United States). It 
continues to have little impact on measures of core inflation, including those tracked by the ECB, such as the HICP; and 
households’ long-term inflation expectations remain anchored. The degree of monetary tightening also differs. In real terms, the 
ECB’s current rates (the deposit facility rate deflated by the core CPI) hover around 0%, a level that more or less corresponds to 
estimates of the natural rate. The Fed, meanwhile, remains in accommodative territory: the real rate (Fed Funds deflated by Core

1 This is a point underscored by the first PPI releases for September (Spain, Italy).

### Page 2

PCE) is at around 0.8%, 40bps below the committee’s implied long-term level (1.2%). Japan remains an outlier: the rise in rates 
preceded the inflationary shock and, until now, has been more a matter of normalization. It is therefore more extensive and spread 
out over time. The central bank is adjusting to the new inflation regime and remains cautious in its exit from accommodative 
policies. The starting point is significantly lower, with real rates still negative, but the adjustment cycle should ultimately bring it to 
the upper end of the neutral range estimated by the BoJ (1.1%-2.5%). We therefore anticipate one final rate hike by the ECB in 
December, two more hikes by the Fed, first in December and then in January, and four spaced-out hikes by the BoJ through the 
second half of 2028.

Anis Bensaidani   
Economist, United States, Japan 
anis.bensaidani@bnpparibas.com

Guillaume Derrien 
Economist, Europe 
guillaume.a.derrien@bnpparibas.com

## Document 299: Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-22).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
09:49 November 13 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest
reading since late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of severe economic stress.
Renewed cost-of-living and interest rate pressures were front and centre following another lift in

inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage
holders are feeling the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group falling to a record low.

### Page 2

Consumers have become noticeably more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the relevant
sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge
up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business survey showed confidence slipped further
to –15 in October as conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic. Of concern for the
outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow materially faster than selling prices, further
compressing already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter financial conditions can jolt activity. The
value of new dwelling finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly decline in the series’
history, taking approvals 25% lower year-to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined with the housing tax changes announced in May.

Owner-occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home buyers proving particularly sensitive
to the deterioration in affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum across the domestic economy,
consistent with Westpac’s expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the

consensus view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the result, but core inflation was
also marginally stronger than forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the past four
months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost
inflation through. Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace, gaining 0.3% in
October.
The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor

Waller prior to the release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and the labour market while
showing significant concern over the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the passthrough
of energy and technology input inflation. His conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the
monthly rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward supporting an increase in our policy
rate of 25 basis points at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI inflation data out

later today are similar to the past two monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent or
even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also kept the focus on inflation risks and
stated or implied the FOMC needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s updates,
pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by
September. We believe this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core inflation ex

shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-month annualised basis and the labour market is
balanced; but it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate, US households are under pressure from
the cost of living, and their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing costs. Highlighting

### Page 3

Related articles
this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and 2010. Our November Market Outlook will
provide a full update on our expectations for the US and the world when released on Westpac IQ this
afternoon.

## Document 311: EcoCharts _ Business creations and bankruptcies - Partial update with monthly data as of September 2026 (2026-11-13)

Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13/11/2026 1
November 2026
BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE: DOUBLE-DIGIT GROWTH IN BUSINESS CREATIONS,
LOWER INCREASE IN BANKRUPTCIES, AND STABLE NON-PERFORMING LOANS – PARTIAL UPDATE WITH
MONTHLY DATA AS OF SEPTEMBER 2026
ECO CHARTS | BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IS A NEW PUBLICATION AIMED AT PROVIDING AN OVERVIEW OF THE DYNAMICS
OF BUSINESS CREATIONS AND BANKRUPTCIES IN FRANCE, AS WELL AS THERISKS ASSOCIATED WITH THE LATTER. WE WILL PUBLISH

REGULAR UPDATES TO THIS PUBLICATION.

### Page 2

Double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans
2ECOCHARTSECONOMIC STUDIES
• Business creations rose by 16% y/y as of Q3 2026, marking an acceleration compared with the growth seen in 2025 (5.3% y/y).
• This increase was driven by sectors associated with new information and communication technologies, where the trend is gaining
momentum.
• Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 1.7% y/y as of Q3 2026, according to the data from the Banque de France (vs. 3.6% y/y in

2025). After a more pronounced rise in the first quarter (8% y/y), this increase has since slowed. This moderation could continue, as,
according to Altares, payment delays have decreased to an average of 12.3 days in the second quarter of 2026 (14.1 days a year
earlier), compared with the European average of 13.1 days.
• The rise in business bankruptcies is most pronounced in the services sector, which has simultaneously seen an increase in business

creations. This trend is clear in the retail sector, with a 26% y/y increase in business creations as of Q3 2026, while business
bankruptcies are up 3% y/y.
• In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been declining since Q2 2026.
• The level of the average ratio of non-performing loans is moderately high and has remained stable for the past year (4.0% of total
loans and advances in Q2 2026). More cyclical than in other sectors, the ratio of non-performing loans in construction reached its

highest level since 2019, at 9.5%.
• The share of underperforming loans, but not yet non-performing (in Stage 2, according to IFRS 9 terminology), which provides an
indication of the future trend in non-performing loans, is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all-time high (16.1%).
• Despite a significant rise since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies — which allows them to be weighted by a proxy for
company size — remains in September, on average, below its Q1 2025 peak (0.54 compared with 0.61, respectively). The economic

weight of bankrupted small businesses decreased but still stands at a high level (2.03).
For more information, please refer to our publication: Chart of the week | French corporates: peaks in creations put peaks in bankruptcies into
perspective. July 22, 2026 – Economic Research – BNP Paribas

Business creations Insee Monthly
Survey of business creations (all types, companies, sole traders, micro-entrepreneurs). Monthly data from

Non-performing loans ratio EBA Quarterly
Gross non performing loans and advances at amortised cost / Total gross loans and advances at
amortised cost
Share of underperforming loans (Stage 2 IFRS 9) ECB Quarterly
Loans and advances in Stage 2 / Total loans and advances. Loans and advances at amortised cost and at
fair value through equity
Economic weight of bankruptcies Banque de France Monthly
Total outstanding bank loans to newly bankrupt NFCs over a one-year period, calculated as the figure for

the month in which the firm is declared bankrupt, relative to the average outstanding bank loans to both
healthy and bankrupt NFCs during the same period.
Share of 90+ days past due payments Urssaf Monthly Contributions still to be collected by Urssaf / Amount of contributions due to Urssaf

## Document 319: European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

European resilience, American vigour

Eurozone growth continues to prove resilient, notwithstanding the volatility of the Irish figures. The Q3 data reinforce 
our forecast of 1.1% growth this year, which could even be slightly exceeded, as German and Spanish momentum shows 
no sign of fading. In France, the negative surprise (-0.1% q/q against a consensus of +0.1% q/q) stems from a new one-
off factor (the sharp fall in aerospace output caused by delays in input deliveries) and masks an encouraging rebound 
in household consumption. In the United States, growth reached its highest level since Q2 2025 (0.8% q/q), driven by 
buoyant private domestic demand.

Eurozone: The resilience of growth is confirmed 
Eurozone GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3 according to Eurostat's preliminary flash estimate, above both the consensus and our 
Nowcast (0.3%). The ever-volatile Irish figures contributed 0.2 pp to this outcome. Excluding Ireland, growth (0.2% q/q) 
nonetheless remains in line with the pace of recent quarters (0.2-0.3% q/q on average). This illustrates the Eurozone's capacity 
to withstand the current shocks, thanks in particular to the strength of Spain (+0.07 pp contribution to the q/q change in Eurozone’s 
GDP) and the Netherlands (+0.04 pp), and to Germany's gradual recovery (+0.06 pp). France (-0.03 pp) and Italy (zero growth), 
by contrast, posted disappointing results, although in France's case these partly reflect temporary factors (aerospace output). The 
growth carry-over for 2026 now stands at 1.0%, which supports our forecast of 1.1% in annual average terms and even points to 
upside risk. The European Commission's October surveys remain upbeat, with a further improvement in industrial activity 
expectations. 
The German recovery continues. As anticipated by our Nowcast, real GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3 (after 0.3% in Q2). 
According to Destatis, consumption (private and public) increased slightly, while investment edged down. Export momentum 
slowed markedly compared with Q2. This is Germany's fourth consecutive quarter of growth. 
Italian GDP surprised on the downside, stagnating in Q3 (against a consensus of 0.1%). According to Istat, this reflects a 
decline in value added in industry, offset by increases in services and agriculture. The positive contribution from domestic demand 
was reportedly cancelled out by a negative contribution from net trade. 
Spanish growth remained robust in Q3, in line with expectations (0.6% q/q, after 0.7% in Q2), driven mainly by domestic 
demand (0.5 pp contribution) and, to a lesser extent, by net exports (0.1 pp). Consumption remains the main engine of growth 
(+0.6% q/q for private consumption and +0.9% for public consumption). Investment rose by 0.7%. Export growth (1.3%) once 
again outpaced import growth (1.1%) this quarter. All sectors except construction recorded positive changes in value added, with 
industry and services both up 0.7%. 
France: A sharp drop in aerospace output prevents a return to growth in Q3 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 (after stagnating in Q2), weighed down by a sharp fall in aerospace output (-4.5% q/q 
in the production of other transport equipment, which includes aerospace). This steep decline dragged down goods production 
(down 1.2% q/q) and explains the sharp contraction in exports (-1.7% q/q) and business investment (-0.4% q/q). Net trade thus 
subtracted 0.6 pp from GDP (imports were flat over the same period). Conversely, household consumption surprised on the 
upside (+0.4% q/q), boosted by purchases of electric vehicles (registrations up 112% y/y in Q3). Public consumption 
(+0.3% q/q) also supported growth, as did activity in market services (+0.3% q/q). Despite the sharp drop in aerospace 
output in Q3, Airbus has confirmed its delivery targets for the year (with engine supply problems expected to be resolved), pointing 
to a strong rebound in growth in Q4 (0.3% q/q according to our forecasts). Overall, growth is expected to reach 0.3% in 2026, 
before picking up to 0.8% in 2027. 
United States: Growth at a one-year high  
GDP growth picked up in Q3 to 3.4% at a seasonally adjusted annualised rate (AR; i.e. 0.8% q/q), from 2.2% in Q2 
(0.6% q/q). This is the fastest pace since Q3 2025, confirming that activity is almost immune to the energy shock. Private domestic 
demand remains particularly strong. Private non-residential investment accelerated to 12.7% AR, a performance unmatched since 
the post-Covid reopening. AI-related components are driving this figure (18.5% AR), but other types of investment are also rising 
for a second consecutive quarter (3.8% AR) after contracting uninterruptedly throughout 2025. Household consumption, steady 
at 2.5% AR in real terms, is growing faster than it did before inflation picked up again, but at the cost of a significant drop in the 
saving rate (3.8% at the end of the quarter, down from 4.4% at the end of the previous quarter). The United States nonetheless 
continues to "import its growth": net trade is weighing on GDP owing to a surge in imports (19.7% AR), driven by AI-related 
components – IT, semiconductors and telecommunications (103.8% AR).

## Document 324: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-22).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-22
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

## Document 354: LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/LatAm’s silicon dividend_ tailwinds from the AI  investment boom in the U.S. (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

LatAm’s silicon dividend: 
tailwinds from the AI  
investment boom in the U.S.

1 
Authors 
Fancisco Campos 
Chief LatAm Economist

Beatriz Garcia Nunes 
LatAm Economist

October 28, 2026 
Key messages: 
- U.S. investment has broken away from Europe and China since 2023, 
powered by AI-related equipment, software and research spending. 
- The AI CapEx boom demands commodities and merchandise that LatAm 
can supply.  
- Latin America’s export gains are real but concentrated. Copper, computer 
and electronics products provide the clearest links to the U.S. AI buildout. 
- Chile is the region’s most direct copper beneficiary. Mexico captures the 
windfall stemming from increases hardware demand and trade-diversion. In 
these cases, the AI-related tailwinds have undergirded appreciable 
improvements in external accounts. 
- The cases for nickel and zinc illustrate that the dividend is not widespread. 
U.S. imports of the former have mostly returned toward pre-pandemic levels, 
while for the latter bilateral gains partly reflect supplier substitution. 
- While expanding AI use, supply shortages and incentives can extend the 
CapEx boom, its financial viability poses downside risks. 
Another ‘American exceptionalism’ story  
Investment has become one of the sharpest dividing lines among the world’s 
core economic blocs. Since 2024, U.S. gross fixed capital formation has firmed 
up appreciably. Meanwhile, investment in Europe has broadly stalled while in 
China’s it has retreated, the drivers of which are beyond the scope of this report 
(Figure 1).  
The U.S. divergence has largely been an AI story: a burst of spending on 
computing power, digital infrastructure and intellectual property has offset 
weakness in conventional construction and housing. Indeed, total U.S. fixed 
investment is nearly 12% above its 2023 level. Within it, investment in 
information-processing equipment has risen by roughly 57%, software outlays by 
about 35%, and research and development by around 15%. By contrast, non-
residential structures and residential investment sit below their 2023 levels 
(Figure 2).

### Page 2

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 2

Figure 1: The global investment divergence

Figure 2: AI-related categories lead U.S. investment

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Eurostat, National Bureau of Statistics of 
China, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: US Bureau of Economic Analysis, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Latin America’s commodity channel 
AI is not merely a semiconductor story. Chips require specialized minerals; data 
centers require electricity and cooling; electricity requires grids, transformers and 
storage. That chain pulls in copper, aluminum, silver, tin, lithium and selected 
semiconductor inputs, along with natural gas and uranium. Latin America has 
meaningful exposure to several of these inputs, but copper has emerged as the 
commodity with the cleanest and most economically significant regional link. 
Copper is used throughout the AI infrastructure stack, including power cables, 
substations, transformers, server boards, heat sinks and cooling systems. Rising 
U.S. copper demand has lifted imports from Chile and Peru, particularly the 
former (Figure 3). The effect matters beyond merchandise export volumes. 
Favorable terms-of-trade have helped Chile generate a current-account surplus 
for the first time in roughly fifteen years (0.2% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-
quarter rolling basis) and have allowed Peru to log the largest surplus in about 
forty-five years (4.5% of GDP as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis).

28 October 2026

### Page 3

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 3

Figure 3: Copper epitomized the commodity link

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Not every metal is copper 
The broader mining story demands more caution, though. U.S. nickel imports have 
recovered toward pre-Covid levels, but the upswing has not displayed the same 
persistence as copper. Mexico has captured a disproportionate share of the 
bilateral improvement, yet that gain looks more like normalization than a 
sustained AI-driven break (Figure 4). Zinc tells a similar story. U.S. real imports 
stand shy of their pre-pandemic levels, and stronger purchases from Mexico and 
Peru partly reflect a reallocation away from Brazil rather than a decisive increase 
in U.S. demand (Figure 5).

Figure 4: Nickel: recovery, not a boom

Figure 5: Zinc: a substitution story

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research

28 October 2026

### Page 4

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 4

Mexico’s hardware advantage 
The larger non-commodity prize is in computers and electronics. U.S. imports of 
these products have sky-rocketed, while the supplier mix has shifted away from 
China. Mexico and various Asian economies have gained share on the back of this 
double-whammy impulse (Figure 6). 
This helps explain why Mexico’s non-automotive manufacturing exports, notably 
electronics, have outpaced automotive shipments to the U.S. and pressures from 
higher oil prices. The resulting non-oil trade surplus has offset pressure from the 
oil balance and helped Mexico generate a balanced current account (0% of GDP 
as of Q2 2026 on a 4-quarter rolling basis). The external boost is meaningful, 
although it should not necessarily be interpreted as bona fide evidence of 
nearshoring, in our view.

Figure 6: U.S. computer & electronics imports going into overdrive

Source: U.S. Census Bureau, Haver Analytics and Deutsche Bank Research.

How much runway remains? 
Quantifying or mapping out a timeframe for the AI investment outlook is beyond 
the scope of this piece or our expertise. Nevertheless, we’d venture to argue that 
the capex cycle appears more likely to mature gradually than to stop abruptly. 
One reason is that the investment case is shifting from frontier-model training 
toward enterprise inference. Capacity that once served development can 
increasingly run commercial workloads for companies. That creates a path to 
revenue even if artificial general intelligence (AGI) crystallizes later than 
expected. It also spreads demand to a much larger corporate customer base.  
A second reason is scarcity. Graphics processing units (GPUs) remain tight, while 
power availability, grid connections, cooling equipment, construction capacity 
and data-center complexity limit how quickly supply can respond. Bottlenecks 
may therefore extend the cycle. The constraint is especially relevant for copper 
28 October 2026

### Page 5

LatAm Thematic Research

Deutsche Bank Securities Inc. Page 5

and power infrastructure, where projects have long lead times and permitting 
hurdles. 
A third reason is strategic. AI development resembles a prisoner’s dilemma at 
both the corporate and national levels. Each participant may worry about costs, 
safety and control, yet slowing unilaterally risks surrendering technological, 
commercial or national security advantages. The same tension is visible in calls 
for guardrails alongside continued spending. Competition can therefore sustain 
capex even when short-term returns are uncertain. 
We acknowledge the risks, nonetheless. Higher borrowing costs raise the hurdle 
rate for an infrastructure buildout financed partly through debt. More important, 
revenues must eventually justify the installed capital base. The bearish case is not 
that AI lacks value; it is that infrastructure spending may run ahead of 
monetization, compromising some companies’ repayment capacity. Public 
opposition to the development of AI infrastructure and/or adoption of AI 
technologies could also slow down the technology’s roll-out. 
The regional balance  
Summing up, the AI investment boom in the U.S. is delivering a dividend to parts 
of Latin America, not the region as a whole. Chile and Peru benefit through copper 
prices and export volumes. Mexico benefits through electronics manufacturing 
and the replacement of Chinese supply. For Brazil and Colombia, the AI dividend 
is rather muted. Where materialized, furthermore, the dividend supports trade 
and current accounts more clearly rather than improving the domestic 
investment. 
These dynamics should persist while U.S. AI investment remains supply-
constrained and strategically protected. If widespread adoption advances and 
the strategic race intensifies, the impulse can last. If financing costs rise further 
or monetization disappoints, the cycle may narrow. Either way, copper and 
Mexican electronics remain the region’s most visible gauges of the U.S. AI 
investment appetite.

28 October 2026

### Page 6

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
LatAm’s silicon dividend: tailwinds from the AI investment boom in the U.S. – October 28, 2026

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary: We retain our near-term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid-term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3-7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15-year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US? Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities among all scenarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer-term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fallible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bonds (CoCos1) 
and US 10-year government bond ideas: We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10-year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:  Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share-price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monetisation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence. We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight, while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long-term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While France’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro 
area bank equities offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5-3.0% q/q, led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased, despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs. Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited, supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as a Core holding, as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weakened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sector contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation, leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk-reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10-year yield fell over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though. Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y. Fed officials have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

Significant share issuance has kept the market

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds, especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell-off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals. The sustained widening of the OAT-Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near-term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the French presidential election. Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near-term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe-haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The France-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves the CHF at a sizeable interest-
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90, excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention. A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

A widening France-Germany 10-year government 
bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than 
credit risk

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near-term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next 12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in French government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustments are weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s 10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premia on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, elevated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi-year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid-2027, reducing USD support and driving the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

France’s fiscal risks constrain near-term EUR/USD 
gains, but a modest recovery remains likely
